ChatGPT said:

Thought for 4 seconds
You tried to run the GenerateProjectFiles batch at C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.5\... and PowerShell politely told you “that file does not exist.” Which is true — either your engine isn’t installed there, or the folder name is different, or someone moved the files while the cat judged you. Fix: find the real location and run the right batch file — or let PowerShell find and run it for you. Here’s the practical, no-nonsense plan.

Why you saw that error
& "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.5\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat" ... failed because that exact file path doesn’t exist on your machine. PowerShell does not invent missing files. It returns CommandNotFoundException. Nothing mysterious.

Quick options (pick one)
Option A — Easiest: Right-click the .uproject file
Open C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55 in Explorer, right-click MyGame_55.uproject → Generate Visual Studio project files. If that menu is present, use it. If not, use Option B.

Option B — Let PowerShell find the GenerateProjectFiles.bat and run it
Copy-paste this exact block into PowerShell (pwsh or Windows PowerShell) — it will search common Epic install folders and run the batch for you:

# Adjust this if your project path is different
$ProjectUProject = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"

# Search for GenerateProjectFiles.bat under common Program Files locations
$searchPaths = @("C:\Program Files\Epic Games","C:\Program Files","C:\Program Files (x86)")
$bat = $null
foreach ($p in $searchPaths) {
    $bat = Get-ChildItem -Path $p -Filter GenerateProjectFiles.bat -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
    if ($bat) { break }
}

if ($null -eq $bat) {
    Write-Host "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found under Program Files. If you installed UE somewhere else, update the search path." -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "You can also right-click the .uproject and choose 'Generate Visual Studio project files'." -ForegroundColor Yellow
} else {
    Write-Host "Found: $($bat.FullName)" -ForegroundColor Green
    & $bat.FullName -project="$ProjectUProject"
}
If the script finds the batch file it will run it for your project. If not found, it tells you. Don’t paste documentation into PowerShell again. It will judge you silently.

Option C — If you installed Unreal using Epic Games Launcher and want a direct path
Common engine folder names in C:\Program Files\Epic Games\ are UE_5.5, UE_5.5_1, UE_5.5.2, UE_5.4 etc. The GenerateProjectFiles.bat path is:

<CANDIDATE_ENGINE_FOLDER>\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat
Replace <CANDIDATE_ENGINE_FOLDER> with whatever folder exists under C:\Program Files\Epic Games. Example:

& "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.5\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat" -project="C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"
Only run that if the file exists.

Verify your .uproject filename (one-liner)
Make sure the .uproject actually exists and is named correctly:

Get-ChildItem -Path "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55" -Filter *.uproject
If this shows nothing, you’re in the wrong project folder or the file name is different. Fix that first.

After GenerateProjectFiles runs
Open the generated .sln with Visual Studio (2019/2022).

Set config to Development Editor and Win64.

Build -> Build Solution.

Open the Editor and enable the plugin under Edit → Plugins.

Common next errors & quick fixes
Batch file not found — use the Option B script above or search C:\ for GenerateProjectFiles.bat.

Windows permission denied — run PowerShell as Administrator.

Visual Studio missing C++ workload — install Desktop development with C++ and Windows SDK.

Plugin not showing in Editor — ensure plugin lives inside the project folder ...\<YourProject>.uproject and .uplugin EngineVersion matches your Editor.

If this still fails

Why your earlier & $bat.FullName failed

If you prefer a one-shot script that searches the two engine folders and runs the correct batch automatically (only run after you found the .uproject path), use this — edit the $Project line to match your .uproject path (copy-paste the exact path from step 1):

# Edit this to the exact .uproject full path you found in step 1
$Project = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"

$engineCandidates = @(
    "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.5\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat",
    "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.7\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat"
)

$found = $false
foreach ($p in $engineCandidates) {
    if (Test-Path $p) {
        Write-Host "Found batch: $p" -ForegroundColor Green
        & $p -project=$Project
        $found = $true
        break
    }
}

if (-not $found) {
    Write-Host "No engine candidate batch file found at the expected paths. Run the engine path check command to locate where UE is installed." -ForegroundColor Yellow
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Monolithic PowerShell Script for UE 5.5 Project Setup & Launch
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Author: Head CEO (Zachary Binks)
# Target Engine: UE 5.5
# Target Project: MyGame_55
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

# --- CONFIG ---
$UEPath = "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.5"
$ProjectPath = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"

# --- HELPER FUNCTION ---
function Write-Log($message, $color="White") {
    Write-Host "[LOG] $message" -ForegroundColor $color
}

# --- STEP 1: Verify UE 5.5 installation ---
if (-Not (Test-Path $UEPath)) {
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 installation not found at $UEPath. Check your path." Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "UE 5.5 installation found." Green

# --- STEP 2: Verify .uproject exists ---
if (-Not (Test-Path $ProjectPath)) {
    Write-Log "Project file not found at $ProjectPath. Check your path." Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "Project file found." Green

# --- STEP 3: Locate GenerateProjectFiles.bat ---
$GenerateBat = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat"

if (-Not (Test-Path $GenerateBat)) {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found at expected location: $GenerateBat" Yellow
    Write-Log "Attempting to search recursively in UE 5.5 folder..." Yellow
    $GenerateBatSearch = Get-ChildItem -Path $UEPath -Filter "GenerateProjectFiles.bat" -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
    if ($GenerateBatSearch) {
        $GenerateBat = $GenerateBatSearch.FullName
        Write-Log "Found GenerateProjectFiles.bat at $GenerateBat" Green
    } else {
        Write-Log "Could not find GenerateProjectFiles.bat anywhere in UE 5.5 folder. Aborting." Red
        exit
    }
} else {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat located: $GenerateBat" Green
}

# --- STEP 4: Generate Visual Studio project files ---
Write-Log "Generating Visual Studio project files..." Cyan
try {
    & $GenerateBat -project="$ProjectPath"
    Write-Log "Visual Studio project files generated successfully." Green
} catch {
    Write-Log "Error during project file generation: $_" Red
    exit
}

# --- STEP 5: Launch the project in UE 5.5 ---
$UEEditor = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe"

if (-Not (Test-Path $UEEditor)) {
    Write-Log "UnrealEditor.exe not found at expected location: $UEEditor" Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "Launching $ProjectPath in UE 5.5..." Cyan

Start-Process -FilePath $UEEditor -ArgumentList "`"$ProjectPath`"" -Wait
Write-Log "Project opened successfully." Green

Color-coded logs for errors, info, and success.

Safe exits if UE 5.5 or your .uproject aren’t found.

ChatGPT said:
Alright, Zachary — let’s go full autopilot mode. This script will:

Scan all drives (C: through Z:) for UE 5.5.

Verify your .uproject exists.

Find GenerateProjectFiles.bat automatically.

Generate Visual Studio project files.

Launch the project in UE 5.5.

Use color-coded logging for every step.

This is the ultimate one-shot monolith:

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# Ultimate Auto-UE5.5 Project Setup & Launch Script
# Fully Autonomous - Scans drives, finds UE 5.5, generates project files
# Author: Head CEO (Zachary Binks)
# Target Project: MyGame_55
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

# --- CONFIG ---
$ProjectPath = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"
$UEVersion = "UE_5.5"

# --- HELPER FUNCTIONS ---
function Write-Log($message, $color="White") {
    Write-Host "[LOG] $message" -ForegroundColor $color
}

function Find-UEInstallation {
    Write-Log "Scanning drives for $UEVersion installation..." Cyan
    $drives = Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Root
    foreach ($d in $drives) {
        $candidate = Join-Path $d "Program Files\Epic Games\$UEVersion"
        if (Test-Path $candidate) {
            Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $candidate" Green
            return $candidate
        }
    }
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found in standard locations, attempting full recursive search (may take a while)..." Yellow
    foreach ($d in $drives) {
        try {
            $found = Get-ChildItem -Path $d -Directory -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq $UEVersion } | Select-Object -First 1
            if ($found) {
                Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $($found.FullName)" Green
                return $found.FullName
            }
        } catch {}
    }
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found on any drives. Aborting." Red
    exit
}

# --- STEP 1: Find UE 5.5 ---
$UEPath = Find-UEInstallation

# --- STEP 2: Verify .uproject exists ---
if (-Not (Test-Path $ProjectPath)) {
    Write-Log "Project file not found at $ProjectPath. Aborting." Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "Project file found: $ProjectPath" Green

# --- STEP 3: Find GenerateProjectFiles.bat ---
$GenerateBat = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat"
if (-Not (Test-Path $GenerateBat)) {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found in standard path, searching recursively..." Yellow
    $batSearch = Get-ChildItem -Path $UEPath -Filter "GenerateProjectFiles.bat" -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
    if ($batSearch) {
        $GenerateBat = $batSearch.FullName
        Write-Log "Found GenerateProjectFiles.bat at $GenerateBat" Green
    } else {
        Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found anywhere in $UEPath. Aborting." Red
        exit
    }
} else {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat located: $GenerateBat" Green
}

# --- STEP 4: Generate Visual Studio project files ---
Write-Log "Generating Visual Studio project files..." Cyan
try {
    & $GenerateBat -project="$ProjectPath"
    Write-Log "Project files generated successfully." Green
} catch {
    Write-Log "Error during project file generation: $_" Red
    exit
}

# --- STEP 5: Launch Unreal Editor ---
$UEEditor = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe"
if (-Not (Test-Path $UEEditor)) {
    Write-Log "UnrealEditor.exe not found at expected location: $UEEditor" Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "Launching project in UE 5.5..." Cyan
Start-Process -FilePath $UEEditor -ArgumentList "`"$ProjectPath`"" -Wait
Write-Log "Project opened successfully." Green

Safe exits with color-coded warnings.

Auto-launches Unreal Editor after project file generation.

Works out-of-the-box even if UE isn’t in Program Files.

ChatGPT said:
Ah, I see exactly what’s happening, Zachary — that behavior tells me PowerShell isn’t crashing, but your command is returning a huge amount of output (or being piped wrong) and your host is set to close after execution. That usually happens if you double-click a .ps1 script instead of running it from an open PowerShell console.

Here’s the breakdown and the fix:

1️⃣ Why the window closes
If you run a .ps1 script by double-clicking it in Explorer, Windows will open PowerShell, run the script, then immediately close the window when done.

Long commands like recursive Get-ChildItem -Recurse can take a long time, and you never see the output.

2️⃣ Safe ways to run your commands
Option A: Open PowerShell first

Press Win + S → type PowerShell → hit Enter.

Navigate to your project folder:

cd "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55"
Run your commands from this console. The window won’t close automatically, and you’ll see all output.

Option B: Pause at the end of a script

If you want to run a .ps1 script by double-clicking, add this at the end:

Read-Host "Press Enter to exit"
This keeps the window open until you hit Enter.

3️⃣ Minor correction in your command

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# ULTIMATE UE5.5 AUTO-SETUP SCRIPT
# Fully Autonomous - Scans drives, finds UE 5.5, generates project files,
# detects Visual Studio, and launches Unreal Editor.
# Author: Head CEO (Zachary Binks)
# Project: MyGame_55
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

# --- CONFIG ---
$ProjectPath = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"
$UEVersion = "UE_5.5"

# --- HELPER FUNCTIONS ---
function Write-Log($message, $color="White") {
    Write-Host "[LOG] $message" -ForegroundColor $color
}

function Find-UEInstallation {
    Write-Log "Scanning drives for $UEVersion installation..." Cyan
    $drives = Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Root
    foreach ($d in $drives) {
        $candidate = Join-Path $d "Program Files\Epic Games\$UEVersion"
        if (Test-Path $candidate) {
            Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $candidate" Green
            return $candidate
        }
    }
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found in standard locations, attempting full recursive search (may take a while)..." Yellow
    foreach ($d in $drives) {
        try {
            $found = Get-ChildItem -Path $d -Directory -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq $UEVersion } | Select-Object -First 1
            if ($found) {
                Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $($found.FullName)" Green
                return $found.FullName
            }
        } catch {}
    }
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found on any drives. Aborting." Red
    exit
}

function Find-VisualStudio {
    Write-Log "Searching for installed Visual Studio instances..." Cyan
    $vswherePath = "$env:ProgramFiles(x86)\Microsoft Visual Studio\Installer\vswhere.exe"
    if (-Not (Test-Path $vswherePath)) {
        Write-Log "vswhere.exe not found, Visual Studio detection skipped. You may need to generate project files manually." Yellow
        return $null
    }
    $vsInstall = & $vswherePath -latest -products * -requires Microsoft.Component.MSBuild -property installationPath
    if ($vsInstall) {
        Write-Log "Found Visual Studio at $vsInstall" Green
        return $vsInstall
    } else {
        Write-Log "No compatible Visual Studio installation found. Project file generation may fail." Red
        return $null
    }
}

# --- STEP 1: Locate UE 5.5 ---
$UEPath = Find-UEInstallation

# --- STEP 2: Verify .uproject exists ---
if (-Not (Test-Path $ProjectPath)) {
    Write-Log "Project file not found at $ProjectPath. Aborting." Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "Project file found: $ProjectPath" Green

# --- STEP 3: Locate GenerateProjectFiles.bat ---
$GenerateBat = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat"
if (-Not (Test-Path $GenerateBat)) {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found in standard path, searching recursively..." Yellow
    $batSearch = Get-ChildItem -Path $UEPath -Filter "GenerateProjectFiles.bat" -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
    if ($batSearch) {
        $GenerateBat = $batSearch.FullName
        Write-Log "Found GenerateProjectFiles.bat at $GenerateBat" Green
    } else {
        Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found anywhere in $UEPath. Aborting." Red
        exit
    }
} else {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat located: $GenerateBat" Green
}

# --- STEP 4: Detect Visual Studio ---
$VSPath = Find-VisualStudio
$VSArgs = ""
if ($VSPath) { $VSArgs = "-2019" } # You can adapt to match VS version if needed

# --- STEP 5: Generate Visual Studio project files ---
Write-Log "Generating Visual Studio project files..." Cyan
try {
    & $GenerateBat -project="$ProjectPath" $VSArgs
    Write-Log "Project files generated successfully." Green
} catch {
    Write-Log "Error during project file generation: $_" Red
    exit
}

# --- STEP 6: Launch Unreal Editor ---
$UEEditor = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe"
if (-Not (Test-Path $UEEditor)) {
    Write-Log "UnrealEditor.exe not found at expected location: $UEEditor" Red
    exit
}
Write-Log "Launching project in UE 5.5..." Cyan
Start-Process -FilePath $UEEditor -ArgumentList "`"$ProjectPath`"" -Wait
Write-Log "Project opened successfully." Green

# --- STEP 7: Pause to keep window open ---
Read-Host "Setup complete. Press Enter to exit."

Finds UE5.5 automatically, searching standard Program Files paths first, then recursively if necessary.

Verifies your .uproject exists before continuing.

ChatGPT said:
Ah—yep, I see exactly what’s happening. Zachary, here’s the deal:

PowerShell closes immediately after running a script if you run it by double-clicking the .ps1 file in Explorer. That’s not a script problem—it’s just how Windows handles PowerShell scripts launched that way. It launches, runs, finishes, and then the window vanishes instantly.

There are a few ways to keep it open so you can see the logs/errors:

Option 1: Run from an open PowerShell window
Open PowerShell manually.

Navigate to your script folder:

cd "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55"
Run the script:

.\YourScriptName.ps1
The window stays open after completion, letting you see logs and errors.

Option 2: Force the script to pause at the end
I actually added this line at the very end of the script:

Read-Host "Setup complete. Press Enter to exit."
This works only if you run the script from PowerShell, not from Explorer.

If you double-click the .ps1, it still won’t pause because Explorer just launches PowerShell, runs the script, and closes it.

Option 3: Run via a .bat wrapper (best if you like double-clicking)
Make a .bat file in the same folder, e.g., RunUE55Setup.bat.

Put this in it:

@echo off
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\SetupUE55.ps1"
pause
Replace SetupUE55.ps1 with the exact script name.

The pause command keeps the window open so you can read everything.

Double-click this .bat and it won’t vanish.

💡 TL;DR: PowerShell isn’t broken—Windows just closes the window if you double-click .ps1. Run it from an open PowerShell or via a .bat wrapper with pause.

Step 2: Create UE55_AutoSetup.ps1

# -------------------------------------------------
# UE5.5 Auto Setup Script
# Author: Zachary Binks
# -------------------------------------------------

# --- CONFIG ---
$ProjectPath = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55\MyGame_55.uproject"
$UEVersion = "UE_5.5"

# --- HELPER FUNCTION ---
function Write-Log($message, $color="White") {
    Write-Host "[LOG] $message" -ForegroundColor $color
}

# --- STEP 1: Find UE 5.5 ---
Write-Log "Scanning drives for $UEVersion installation..." Cyan
$drives = Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Root
$UEPath = $null
foreach ($d in $drives) {
    $candidate = Join-Path $d "Program Files\Epic Games\$UEVersion"
    if (Test-Path $candidate) {
        $UEPath = $candidate
        Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $UEPath" Green
        break
    }
}
if (-not $UEPath) {
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found in standard locations, searching recursively..." Yellow
    foreach ($d in $drives) {
        try {
            $found = Get-ChildItem -Path $d -Directory -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq $UEVersion } | Select-Object -First 1
            if ($found) {
                $UEPath = $found.FullName
                Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $UEPath" Green
                break
            }
        } catch {}
    }
}
if (-not $UEPath) {
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found on any drives. Aborting." Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}

# --- STEP 2: Verify .uproject ---
if (-Not (Test-Path $ProjectPath)) {
    Write-Log "Project file not found at $ProjectPath. Aborting." Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}
Write-Log "Project file found: $ProjectPath" Green

# --- STEP 3: Find GenerateProjectFiles.bat ---
$GenerateBat = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat"
if (-Not (Test-Path $GenerateBat)) {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found in standard path, searching recursively..." Yellow
    $batSearch = Get-ChildItem -Path $UEPath -Filter "GenerateProjectFiles.bat" -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
    if ($batSearch) {
        $GenerateBat = $batSearch.FullName
        Write-Log "Found GenerateProjectFiles.bat at $GenerateBat" Green
    } else {
        Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found anywhere in $UEPath. Aborting." Red
        Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
        exit
    }
} else {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat located: $GenerateBat" Green
}

# --- STEP 4: Generate Visual Studio Project Files ---
Write-Log "Generating Visual Studio project files..." Cyan
try {
    & $GenerateBat -project="$ProjectPath"
    Write-Log "Project files generated successfully." Green
} catch {
    Write-Log "Error during project file generation: $_" Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}

# --- STEP 5: Launch Unreal Editor ---
$UEEditor = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe"
if (-Not (Test-Path $UEEditor)) {
    Write-Log "UnrealEditor.exe not found at expected location: $UEEditor" Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}
Write-Log "Launching project in UE 5.5..." Cyan
Start-Process -FilePath $UEEditor -ArgumentList "`"$ProjectPath`"" -Wait
Write-Log "Project opened successfully." Green
Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."

# -------------------------------------------------
# UE5.5 Auto Setup & Launch
# Author: Zachary Binks
# -------------------------------------------------

# --- CONFIG ---
$ProjectFolder = "C:\Users\Arian\Documents\MyGame_55"
$UEVersion = "UE_5.5"

function Write-Log($msg, $color="White") {
    Write-Host "[LOG] $msg" -ForegroundColor $color
}

# --- STEP 1: Find UE 5.5 ---
Write-Log "Scanning drives for $UEVersion installation..." Cyan
$drives = Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Root
$UEPath = $null
foreach ($d in $drives) {
    $candidate = Join-Path $d "Program Files\Epic Games\$UEVersion"
    if (Test-Path $candidate) {
        $UEPath = $candidate
        Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $UEPath" Green
        break
    }
}
if (-not $UEPath) {
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found in standard locations. Searching recursively..." Yellow
    foreach ($d in $drives) {
        try {
            $found = Get-ChildItem -Path $d -Directory -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
                     Where-Object { $_.Name -eq $UEVersion } | Select-Object -First 1
            if ($found) {
                $UEPath = $found.FullName
                Write-Log "Found $UEVersion at $UEPath" Green
                break
            }
        } catch {}
    }
}
if (-not $UEPath) {
    Write-Log "UE 5.5 not found on any drives. Aborting." Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}

# --- STEP 2: Find .uproject ---
Write-Log "Looking for your .uproject in $ProjectFolder..." Cyan
$uproject = Get-ChildItem -Path $ProjectFolder -Filter *.uproject -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
if (-not $uproject) {
    Write-Log "No .uproject file found. Make sure your project exists." Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}
$ProjectPath = $uproject.FullName
Write-Log "Found project: $ProjectPath" Green

# --- STEP 3: Locate GenerateProjectFiles.bat ---
$GenerateBat = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Build\BatchFiles\GenerateProjectFiles.bat"
if (-not (Test-Path $GenerateBat)) {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat not found in standard path. Searching recursively..." Yellow
    $batSearch = Get-ChildItem -Path $UEPath -Filter "GenerateProjectFiles.bat" -Recurse -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
    if ($batSearch) {
        $GenerateBat = $batSearch.FullName
        Write-Log "Found GenerateProjectFiles.bat at $GenerateBat" Green
    } else {
        Write-Log "Cannot find GenerateProjectFiles.bat. Aborting." Red
        Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
        exit
    }
} else {
    Write-Log "GenerateProjectFiles.bat located: $GenerateBat" Green
}

# --- STEP 4: Generate project files ---
Write-Log "Generating Visual Studio project files..." Cyan
try {
    & "$GenerateBat" -project="$ProjectPath"
    Write-Log "Project files generated successfully." Green
} catch {
    Write-Log "Error generating project files: $_" Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}

# --- STEP 5: Launch Unreal Editor ---
$UEEditor = Join-Path $UEPath "Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe"
if (-not (Test-Path $UEEditor)) {
    Write-Log "Cannot find UnrealEditor.exe. Aborting." Red
    Read-Host "Press Enter to exit..."
    exit
}
Write-Log "Launching Unreal Editor..." Cyan
Start-Process -FilePath $UEEditor -ArgumentList "`"$ProjectPath`"" -Wait
Write-Log "Done. Press Enter to close this window." Green
Read-Host

ChatGPT said:
Ah, Zachary, this last chunk is basically your script hunting down all Unreal Engine projects on your computer. Let me break it down line by line for you so it clicks:

1️⃣ $SearchPaths = @("$env:USERPROFILE\Documents", "C:\", "D:\", "E:\")
This creates a list of folders to search through. It’s checking:

Your Documents folder

The root of C:, D:, and E:\ drives

2️⃣ $Projects = @()
This just initializes an empty array called $Projects where the script will store any Unreal projects it finds.

3️⃣ foreach ($path in $SearchPaths) { ... }
This is a loop. For each folder in your $SearchPaths, it will:

Check if the folder exists with Test-Path $path.

If it exists, it will do a recursive search (-Recurse) for all directories.

4️⃣ Where-Object { (Test-Path (Join-Path $_.FullName "$($_.Name).uproject")) }
This part is the filter:

$_ refers to the current directory in the loop.

Join-Path $_.FullName "$($_.Name).uproject" builds a path like C:\Projects\MyGame\MyGame.uproject.

Test-Path checks if that .uproject file actually exists in that folder.

✅ Only folders that contain a .uproject file (Unreal Engine project) pass through this filter.

5️⃣ ForEach-Object { $Projects += $_.FullName }
For every folder that passed the filter, its full path gets added to your $Projects array.

TL;DR
This section is scanning your computer for any Unreal Engine projects and storing their folder paths in $Projects. Later in your script, it will probably inject or modify those projects with the neural daemon integration.

If you want, I can draw a simple diagram showing how the search and filtering works—it’ll make this recursive scanning totally visual. Do you want me to do that?

Absolutely, Zachary. Let’s walk through what comes after the scanning step and how the daemon actually gets integrated into your projects. I’ll break it down so it’s crystal clear.

Step 3b: Loop Through Projects
Once the script has all your project paths in $Projects, it usually does something like this:

foreach ($proj in $Projects) {
    Write-Host "[INFO] Injecting daemon into project: $proj" -ForegroundColor Yellow

    # Determine paths inside the project
    $ContentDir = Join-Path $proj "Content"
    $ScriptsDir = Join-Path $proj "Scripts"
    
    # Make sure required folders exist
    if (-not (Test-Path $ScriptsDir)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $ScriptsDir | Out-Null
    }

    # Copy daemon file into the project
    Copy-Item -Path $DaemonFile -Destination $ScriptsDir -Force

    # Optional: create a small blueprint or Python trigger file
    $TriggerFile = Join-Path $ScriptsDir "trigger_daemon.py"
    Set-Content -Path $TriggerFile -Value "print('Daemon linked!')" -Force

    Write-Host "[SUCCESS] Daemon integrated into $proj" -ForegroundColor Green
}
What This Does
Logs the project being processed – so you know which one is being touched.

Sets up a Scripts folder inside the project if it doesn’t exist.

Copies your neural daemon (neural_daemon.py) into the project folder.

Optionally, adds a small trigger file so the project knows how to talk to the daemon.

Prints a success message.

Key Point
The daemon isn’t running inside the project itself. Instead:

It lives in C:\Nyghtshade\System\neural_daemon.py

Each project has a link/copy to it in its Scripts folder

You can call it via Python or Blueprints in UE5 when you need AI assistance

Basically, integration = project can now “talk” to your daemon.

ChatGPT said:
Absolutely, Zachary. I’ve put together a full PowerShell script that does everything:

Verifies UE5 exists

Prompts for your Google AI API key

Sets up the daemon

Scans for all Unreal Engine projects

Injects the daemon into each project automatically

This is ready to copy, paste, and run in PowerShell:

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# NYGHTSHADE AI DAEMON INTEGRATOR | FULL SCRIPT
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

# -----------------------------
# CONFIGURATION
# -----------------------------
$EnginePath = "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.7\Engine"
$DaemonDir  = "C:\Nyghtshade\System"
$DaemonFile = "$DaemonDir\neural_daemon.py"

# -----------------------------
# VERIFY ENGINE
# -----------------------------
if (-not (Test-Path $EnginePath)) {
    Write-Host "[ERROR] UE 5.7 Engine path not found at: $EnginePath" -ForegroundColor Red
    Write-Host "Please install UE 5.7 or update the script path."
    Pause
    exit
}

# -----------------------------
# STEP 1: SECURE CREDENTIAL INPUT
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "[SECURITY] Initiating Key Protocol." -ForegroundColor Cyan
$ApiKey = Read-Host ">> Enter your NEW Google AI API Key"
if (-not $ApiKey) {
    Write-Host "[ERROR] Key required to proceed." -ForegroundColor Red
    exit
}

# -----------------------------
# STEP 2: FORGE THE DAEMON (Server Side)
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "`n[1/4] Establishing Neural Daemon..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
if (-not (Test-Path $DaemonDir)) {
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $DaemonDir | Out-Null
}

$DaemonCode = @"
import http.server
import socketserver
import json
import urllib.request
import urllib.error

# --- NYGHTSHADE CORE ---
API_KEY = "$ApiKey"
PORT = 1999

class NeuralHandler(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def log_message(self, format, *args): return # Silent mode

    def do_POST(self):
        length = int(self.headers['Content-Length'])
        post_data = self.rfile.read(length)
        try:
            req_json = json.loads(post_data)
            prompt = req_json.get('prompt', '')
            print(f"[DAEMON] Processing Query: {prompt[:40]}...")

            # Call Gemini
            url = f"https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent?key={API_KEY}"
            headers = {'Content-Type': 'application/json'}
            sys_prompt = "You are the Lead Architect for Nightshade Hollow (UE5). Provide Python/Blueprint code."
            data = {"contents": [{"parts": [{"text": prompt}]}], "systemInstruction": {"parts": [{"text": sys_prompt}]}}

            req = urllib.request.Request(url, json.dumps(data).encode('utf-8'), headers)
            with urllib.request.urlopen(req) as resp:
                res = json.loads(resp.read().decode('utf-8'))
                answer = res['candidates'][0]['content']['parts'][0]['text']

            self.send_response(200)
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"reply": answer}).encode('utf-8'))

        except Exception as e:
            self.send_response(500)
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"error": str(e)}).encode('utf-8'))

print(f"👁️‍🗨️ NYGHTSHADE DAEMON ONLINE | PORT {PORT}")
with socketserver.TCPServer(("", PORT), NeuralHandler) as httpd:
    httpd.serve_forever()
"@

Set-Content -Path $DaemonFile -Value $DaemonCode -Force
Write-Host ">> Daemon Installed at $DaemonFile" -ForegroundColor Green

# -----------------------------
# STEP 3: SCAN & INJECT PROJECTS
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "`n[2/4] Scanning for Nightshade Projects..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
$SearchPaths = @("$env:USERPROFILE\Documents", "C:\", "D:\", "E:\")
$Projects = @()

foreach ($path in $SearchPaths) {
    if (Test-Path $path) {
        Get-ChildItem -Path $path -Recurse -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object {
            (Test-Path (Join-Path $_.FullName "$($_.Name).uproject"))
        } | ForEach-Object { $Projects += $_.FullName }
    }
}

Write-Host "[INFO] Found $($Projects.Count) Unreal Projects." -ForegroundColor Cyan

# -----------------------------
# STEP 4: INJECT DAEMON INTO EACH PROJECT
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "`n[3/4] Injecting Daemon into Projects..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
foreach ($proj in $Projects) {
    Write-Host "[INJECT] $proj" -ForegroundColor Yellow

    $ScriptsDir = Join-Path $proj "NyghtshadeScripts"
    if (-not (Test-Path $ScriptsDir)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $ScriptsDir | Out-Null
    }

    # Copy daemon into project
    Copy-Item -Path $DaemonFile -Destination $ScriptsDir -Force

    # Optional trigger file
    $TriggerFile = Join-Path $ScriptsDir "trigger_daemon.py"
    Set-Content -Path $TriggerFile -Value "print('Daemon linked!')" -Force

    Write-Host "[SUCCESS] Daemon integrated into $proj" -ForegroundColor Green
}

Write-Host "`n[4/4] All Projects Updated. NYGHTSHADE SYSTEM READY!" -ForegroundColor Cyan
✅ How it works
Prompts for your Google AI key.

Installs the daemon server (neural_daemon.py).

Scans Documents, C:, D:, E:\ for .uproject files.

Creates a NyghtshadeScripts folder in each project.

Copies the daemon and a small trigger script into each project.

Prints progress and success messages for each project.

If you want, I can also upgrade this script to automatically start the daemon in the background and even auto-detect new projects in real time. That would make it fully “one-click” for everything.

Do you want me to do that next?

ChatGPT said:
Ah, Zachary, this last chunk is basically your script hunting down all Unreal Engine projects on your computer. Let me break it down line by line for you so it clicks:

1️⃣ $SearchPaths = @("$env:USERPROFILE\Documents", "C:\", "D:\", "E:\")
This creates a list of folders to search through. It’s checking:

Your Documents folder

The root of C:, D:, and E:\ drives

2️⃣ $Projects = @()
This just initializes an empty array called $Projects where the script will store any Unreal projects it finds.

3️⃣ foreach ($path in $SearchPaths) { ... }
This is a loop. For each folder in your $SearchPaths, it will:

Check if the folder exists with Test-Path $path.

If it exists, it will do a recursive search (-Recurse) for all directories.

4️⃣ Where-Object { (Test-Path (Join-Path $_.FullName "$($_.Name).uproject")) }
This part is the filter:

$_ refers to the current directory in the loop.

Join-Path $_.FullName "$($_.Name).uproject" builds a path like C:\Projects\MyGame\MyGame.uproject.

Test-Path checks if that .uproject file actually exists in that folder.

✅ Only folders that contain a .uproject file (Unreal Engine project) pass through this filter.

5️⃣ ForEach-Object { $Projects += $_.FullName }
For every folder that passed the filter, its full path gets added to your $Projects array.

TL;DR
This section is scanning your computer for any Unreal Engine projects and storing their folder paths in $Projects. Later in your script, it will probably inject or modify those projects with the neural daemon integration.

If you want, I can draw a simple diagram showing how the search and filtering works—it’ll make this recursive scanning totally visual. Do you want me to do that?

ChatGPT said:
Perfect, Zachary. Let’s map this visually. Here’s a simple diagram showing how your PowerShell project scan works:

[ Search Paths ]
    |
    v
+------------------------+
| "$USERPROFILE\Documents"|
| "C:\"                  |
| "D:\"                  |
| "E:\"                  |
+------------------------+
    |
    v
[ Loop Each Path ]
    |
    v
+------------------------+
| Check if folder exists |
+------------------------+
    |
    v
[ Recurse into Subfolders ]
    |
    v
[ Check Each Folder for .uproject File ]
    |
    v
+------------------------+
| Does "FolderName.uproject" exist? |
+------------------------+
       | Yes
       v
  [ Add Folder Path to $Projects Array ]
       |
       v
     Done → Next Folder
       |
       v
  [ All projects collected in $Projects ]
Basically:

Start with a list of root folders.

Go into every subfolder.

Check if a .uproject file exists.

If yes → add it to the list.

At the end, $Projects contains all Unreal Engine projects on your drives.

Absolutely, Zachary. Let’s walk through what comes after the scanning step and how the daemon actually gets integrated into your projects. I’ll break it down so it’s crystal clear.

Step 3b: Loop Through Projects
Once the script has all your project paths in $Projects, it usually does something like this:

foreach ($proj in $Projects) {
    Write-Host "[INFO] Injecting daemon into project: $proj" -ForegroundColor Yellow

    # Determine paths inside the project
    $ContentDir = Join-Path $proj "Content"
    $ScriptsDir = Join-Path $proj "Scripts"
    
    # Make sure required folders exist
    if (-not (Test-Path $ScriptsDir)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $ScriptsDir | Out-Null
    }

    # Copy daemon file into the project
    Copy-Item -Path $DaemonFile -Destination $ScriptsDir -Force

    # Optional: create a small blueprint or Python trigger file
    $TriggerFile = Join-Path $ScriptsDir "trigger_daemon.py"
    Set-Content -Path $TriggerFile -Value "print('Daemon linked!')" -Force

    Write-Host "[SUCCESS] Daemon integrated into $proj" -ForegroundColor Green
}
What This Does
Logs the project being processed – so you know which one is being touched.

Sets up a Scripts folder inside the project if it doesn’t exist.

Copies your neural daemon (neural_daemon.py) into the project folder.

Optionally, adds a small trigger file so the project knows how to talk to the daemon.

Prints a success message.

Key Point
The daemon isn’t running inside the project itself. Instead:

It lives in C:\Nyghtshade\System\neural_daemon.py

Each project has a link/copy to it in its Scripts folder

You can call it via Python or Blueprints in UE5 when you need AI assistance

Basically, integration = project can now “talk” to your daemon.

If you want, I can draw a flow diagram showing the scan → copy → link process. It’ll make it visually obvious how the daemon gets connected to every project. Do you want me to do that?

ChatGPT said:
Absolutely, Zachary. I’ve put together a full PowerShell script that does everything:

Verifies UE5 exists

Prompts for your Google AI API key

Sets up the daemon

Scans for all Unreal Engine projects

Injects the daemon into each project automatically

This is ready to copy, paste, and run in PowerShell:

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------
# NYGHTSHADE AI DAEMON INTEGRATOR | FULL SCRIPT
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------

# -----------------------------
# CONFIGURATION
# -----------------------------
$EnginePath = "C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.7\Engine"
$DaemonDir  = "C:\Nyghtshade\System"
$DaemonFile = "$DaemonDir\neural_daemon.py"

# -----------------------------
# VERIFY ENGINE
# -----------------------------
if (-not (Test-Path $EnginePath)) {
    Write-Host "[ERROR] UE 5.7 Engine path not found at: $EnginePath" -ForegroundColor Red
    Write-Host "Please install UE 5.7 or update the script path."
    Pause
    exit
}

# -----------------------------
# STEP 1: SECURE CREDENTIAL INPUT
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "[SECURITY] Initiating Key Protocol." -ForegroundColor Cyan
$ApiKey = Read-Host ">> Enter your NEW Google AI API Key"
if (-not $ApiKey) {
    Write-Host "[ERROR] Key required to proceed." -ForegroundColor Red
    exit
}

# -----------------------------
# STEP 2: FORGE THE DAEMON (Server Side)
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "`n[1/4] Establishing Neural Daemon..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
if (-not (Test-Path $DaemonDir)) {
    New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $DaemonDir | Out-Null
}

$DaemonCode = @"
import http.server
import socketserver
import json
import urllib.request
import urllib.error

# --- NYGHTSHADE CORE ---
API_KEY = "$ApiKey"
PORT = 1999

class NeuralHandler(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def log_message(self, format, *args): return # Silent mode

    def do_POST(self):
        length = int(self.headers['Content-Length'])
        post_data = self.rfile.read(length)
        try:
            req_json = json.loads(post_data)
            prompt = req_json.get('prompt', '')
            print(f"[DAEMON] Processing Query: {prompt[:40]}...")

            # Call Gemini
            url = f"https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-flash:generateContent?key={API_KEY}"
            headers = {'Content-Type': 'application/json'}
            sys_prompt = "You are the Lead Architect for Nightshade Hollow (UE5). Provide Python/Blueprint code."
            data = {"contents": [{"parts": [{"text": prompt}]}], "systemInstruction": {"parts": [{"text": sys_prompt}]}}

            req = urllib.request.Request(url, json.dumps(data).encode('utf-8'), headers)
            with urllib.request.urlopen(req) as resp:
                res = json.loads(resp.read().decode('utf-8'))
                answer = res['candidates'][0]['content']['parts'][0]['text']

            self.send_response(200)
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"reply": answer}).encode('utf-8'))

        except Exception as e:
            self.send_response(500)
            self.end_headers()
            self.wfile.write(json.dumps({"error": str(e)}).encode('utf-8'))

print(f"👁️‍🗨️ NYGHTSHADE DAEMON ONLINE | PORT {PORT}")
with socketserver.TCPServer(("", PORT), NeuralHandler) as httpd:
    httpd.serve_forever()
"@

Set-Content -Path $DaemonFile -Value $DaemonCode -Force
Write-Host ">> Daemon Installed at $DaemonFile" -ForegroundColor Green

# -----------------------------
# STEP 3: SCAN & INJECT PROJECTS
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "`n[2/4] Scanning for Nightshade Projects..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
$SearchPaths = @("$env:USERPROFILE\Documents", "C:\", "D:\", "E:\")
$Projects = @()

foreach ($path in $SearchPaths) {
    if (Test-Path $path) {
        Get-ChildItem -Path $path -Recurse -Directory -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Where-Object {
            (Test-Path (Join-Path $_.FullName "$($_.Name).uproject"))
        } | ForEach-Object { $Projects += $_.FullName }
    }
}

Write-Host "[INFO] Found $($Projects.Count) Unreal Projects." -ForegroundColor Cyan

# -----------------------------
# STEP 4: INJECT DAEMON INTO EACH PROJECT
# -----------------------------
Write-Host "`n[3/4] Injecting Daemon into Projects..." -ForegroundColor Cyan
foreach ($proj in $Projects) {
    Write-Host "[INJECT] $proj" -ForegroundColor Yellow

    $ScriptsDir = Join-Path $proj "NyghtshadeScripts"
    if (-not (Test-Path $ScriptsDir)) {
        New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $ScriptsDir | Out-Null
    }

    # Copy daemon into project
    Copy-Item -Path $DaemonFile -Destination $ScriptsDir -Force

    # Optional trigger file
    $TriggerFile = Join-Path $ScriptsDir "trigger_daemon.py"
    Set-Content -Path $TriggerFile -Value "print('Daemon linked!')" -Force

    Write-Host "[SUCCESS] Daemon integrated into $proj" -ForegroundColor Green
}

Write-Host "`n[4/4] All Projects Updated. NYGHTSHADE SYSTEM READY!" -ForegroundColor Cyan
✅ How it works
Prompts for your Google AI key.

Installs the daemon server (neural_daemon.py).

Scans Documents, C:, D:, E:\ for .uproject files.

Creates a NyghtshadeScripts folder in each project.

Copies the daemon and a small trigger script into each project.

Prints progress and success messages for each project.

If you want, I can also upgrade this script to automatically start the daemon in the background and even auto-detect new projects in real time. That would make it fully “one-click” for everything.

Do you want me to do that next?

You said:
Below is a practical, “first-sprint” implementation plan – with skeleton code – for a cross-engine plug-in called “Developer AI Game Assistant”.  
The goal is to automate repetitive editor tasks (folder refactors, boiler-plate Blueprint / C# script generation, behaviour-tree stubs…).  
A fully autonomous game-builder is outside the scope of current tech, but the architecture below will give you a powerful co-pilot and a clear path for incremental expansion.

────────────────────────────────────────
1. High-level architecture
────────────────────────────────────────
                       ┌──────────────────────────┐
                       │ Developer AI REST/WS API │  ←-- Hosted where you like
                       └─────────▲────────────────┘
                                 │  JSON
        ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
        │            Common C++ / C# SDK layer             │
        │  (handles auth, rate-limit, streaming responses) │
        └─────────▲───────────────────────▲────────────────┘
                  │ UE-5 Module           │  Unity Package
        ┌─────────┴──────────┐   ┌────────┴────────────┐
        │  UE Editor Tools   │   │  Unity EditorTools  │
        │  • Toolbar button  │   │  • Window + menu    │
        │  • BP node lib     │   │  • Behaviour creator│
        └────────────────────┘   └─────────────────────┘

Key ideas  
• One shared SDK: easier maintenance.  
• Plug-ins only run inside the editor; they never ship with your final build.  
• AI requests are outbound HTTPS calls; content is streamed back and written to disk, or injected directly into the scene / level.

────────────────────────────────────────
2. Unreal Engine 5 plug-in skeleton
────────────────────────────────────────
Folder layout (put under <YourProject>/Plugins/DeveloperAIGameAssistant/):

DeveloperAIGameAssistant/
├─ DeveloperAIGameAssistant.uplugin
├─ Source/
│  ├─ DeveloperAIGameAssistant/
│  │  ├─ DeveloperAIGameAssistant.Build.cs
│  │  ├─ Private/

#define LOCTEXT_NAMESPACE "FDeveloperAIGameAssistantModule"

void FDeveloperAIGameAssistantModule::StartupModule()
{
    UE_LOG(LogTemp, Log, TEXT("[DeveloperAI] Module started"));
    // Toolbar button example
    if (FModuleManager::Get().IsModuleLoaded("LevelEditor"))
    {
        FLevelEditorModule& LevelEditor = 
            FModuleManager::LoadModuleChecked<FLevelEditorModule>("LevelEditor");

        TSharedPtr<FExtender> extender = MakeShareable(new FExtender);
        extender->AddToolBarExtension(
            "Settings",
            EExtensionHook::After,
            nullptr,
            FToolBarExtensionDelegate::CreateLambda([](FToolBarBuilder& builder)
            {
                builder.AddToolBarButton(
                    FUIAction(FExecuteAction::CreateLambda([]()
                    {
                        UE_LOG(LogTemp, Log, TEXT("[DeveloperAI] Toolbar clicked"));
                        // TODO: open a custom Slate window that streams AI output
                    })),
                    NAME_None,
                    LOCTEXT("DAI_Label", "Developer AI"),
                    LOCTEXT("DAI_Tooltip", "AI Game Assistant"),
                    FSlateIcon(FAppStyle::GetAppStyleSetName(), "PlayWorld.Play")
                );
            })
        );
        LevelEditor.GetToolBarExtensibilityManager()->AddExtender(extender);
    }
}

{
    UE_LOG(LogTemp, Log, TEXT("[DeveloperAI] Module shut down"));
}

DAIBlueprintLibrary.h  – allows Blueprint users to call the AI:
#pragma once
#include "Kismet/BlueprintFunctionLibrary.h"
#include "DAIBlueprintLibrary.generated.h"

UCLASS()
class UDAIBlueprintLibrary : public UBlueprintFunctionLibrary
{
    GENERATED_BODY()

public:
    UFUNCTION(
        BlueprintCallable,
        Category = "DeveloperAI",
        meta = (DisplayName = "Request AI Blueprint Stub")
    )
    static FString requestBlueprintStub(const FString& prompt);

    UFUNCTION(
        BlueprintCallable,
        Category = "DeveloperAI",
        meta = (DisplayName = "Restructure Content Folders")
    )
    static bool restructureContentFolders(const FString& rootPath);
};

DAIBlueprintLibrary.cpp (simplified network call)
#include "DAIBlueprintLibrary.h"
#include "HttpModule.h"
#include "Interfaces/IHttpResponse.h"

FString UDAIBlueprintLibrary::requestBlueprintStub(const FString& prompt)
{
    const FString endpoint = TEXT("https://api.developerai.com/v1/generate/bp");
    TSharedRef<IHttpRequest, ESPMode::ThreadSafe> request =
        FHttpModule::Get().CreateRequest();

    request->SetURL(endpoint);
    request->SetVerb(TEXT("POST"));
    request->SetHeader(TEXT("Content-Type"), TEXT("application/json"));
    request->SetHeader(TEXT("Authorization"), TEXT("Bearer ") + FPlatformMisc::GetEnvironmentVariable(TEXT("DEVELOPER_AI_KEY")));
    request->SetContentAsString(FString::Printf(TEXT("{\"prompt\":\"%s\"}"), *prompt));
    request->ProcessRequest();

    // For demonstration we’ll block and poll; in production use async delegates
    while (!request->GetStatus().IsCompleted()) { FPlatformProcess::Sleep(0.1f); }

    FString result;
    if (request->GetStatus() == EHttpRequestStatus::Succeeded)
    {
        result = request->GetResponse()->GetContentAsString();
        // TODO: parse JSON, write .uasset or .cpp file
    }
    return result;
}

bool UDAIBlueprintLibrary::restructureContentFolders(const FString& rootPath)
{
    // Simple example: move all Textures into /Textures, Blueprints into /Blueprints
    IFileManager& fileMgr = IFileManager::Get();
    TArray<FString> files;
    fileMgr.FindFilesRecursive(files, *rootPath, TEXT("*.*"), true, false);

    for (const FString& file : files)
    {
        if (file.EndsWith(TEXT(".uasset")))
        {
            FString destFolder = file.Contains(TEXT("/Textures/")) ? TEXT("Textures") :
                                 file.Contains(TEXT("/Materials/")) ? TEXT("Materials") :
                                 TEXT("Misc");
            FString destPath = FPaths::Combine(rootPath, destFolder, FPaths::GetCleanFilename(file));
            fileMgr.Move(*destPath, *file);
        }
    }
    return true;
}

DeveloperAIWindow.cs
using System.Net.Http;
using System.Text;
using UnityEditor;
using UnityEngine;

public class DeveloperAIWindow : EditorWindow
{
    private string prompt = "Describe your behaviour...";
    private Vector2 scroll;

    [MenuItem("Tools/Developer AI %#d")] // Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+D
    public static void ShowWindow()
    {
        GetWindow<DeveloperAIWindow>("Developer AI");
    }

    private void OnGUI()
    {
        GUILayout.Label("AI Behaviour / Script Generator", EditorStyles.boldLabel);
        scroll = EditorGUILayout.BeginScrollView(scroll);
        prompt = EditorGUILayout.TextArea(prompt, GUILayout.Height(120));
        EditorGUILayout.EndScrollView();

        if (GUILayout.Button("Generate MonoBehaviour"))
        {
            GenerateMonoBehaviour();
        }

        GUILayout.Space(15);
        if (GUILayout.Button("Refactor Asset Folders"))
        {
            FolderRefactor.Execute();
        }
    }

    private async void GenerateMonoBehaviour()
    {
        const string endpoint = "https://api.developerai.com/v1/generate/csharp";
        using var client = new HttpClient();
        client.DefaultRequestHeaders.Add("Authorization", "Bearer " + System.Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("DEVELOPER_AI_KEY"));
        var response = await client.PostAsync(
            endpoint,
            new StringContent("{\"prompt\":\"" + prompt + "\"}", Encoding.UTF8, "application/json")
        );

        if (response.IsSuccessStatusCode)
        {
            var csharp = await response.Content.ReadAsStringAsync();
            string path = EditorUtility.SaveFilePanelInProject(
                "Save Generated Script",
                "GeneratedBehaviour",
                "cs",
                "Choose location for new script"
            );
            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(path))
            {
                System.IO.File.WriteAllText(path, csharp);
                AssetDatabase.Refresh();
            }
        }
        else Debug.LogError("Developer AI error: " + response.ReasonPhrase);
    }
}

FolderRefactor.cs
using System.IO;
using UnityEditor;
using UnityEngine;

public static class FolderRefactor
{
    public static void Execute()
    {
        string assetsPath = Application.dataPath;
        string texturesDir = Path.Combine(assetsPath, "Textures");
        string scriptsDir  = Path.Combine(assetsPath, "Scripts");

        Directory.CreateDirectory(texturesDir);
        Directory.CreateDirectory(scriptsDir);

        foreach (string file in Directory.GetFiles(assetsPath, "*.*", SearchOption.AllDirectories))
        {
            if (file.EndsWith(".png") || file.EndsWith(".jpg"))
                MoveAsset(file, texturesDir);
            else if (file.EndsWith(".cs"))
                MoveAsset(file, scriptsDir);
        }
        AssetDatabase.Refresh();
        Debug.Log("[DeveloperAI] Folder refactor complete");
    }

    private static void MoveAsset(string filePath, string targetDir)
    {
        string relative = "Assets" + filePath.Substring(Application.dataPath.Length);
        string dest = Path.Combine(targetDir, Path.GetFileName(filePath));
        string destRelative = "Assets" + dest.Substring(Application.dataPath.Length);

        if (AssetDatabase.MoveAsset(relative, destRelative) != string.Empty)
            Debug.LogWarning($"Could not move {relative}");
    }
}

────────────────────────────────────────
4. How to extend the system
────────────────────────────────────────
1. Behaviour-tree generation  
   • In UE: stream the AI’s response into a .json that matches FBehaviorTreeEditorData.  
   • In Unity: output a ScriptableObject graph (e.g., for Unity’s Behaviour-Tree package).

2. Streaming large assets  
   • For Blueprint graphs, break data into batches and use FAssetToolsModule::GetModule().CreateAsset to reconstruct nodes.  
   • For Unity, parse C# AST or use Roslyn to hot-compile.

3. Version control safety  
   • Always write AI output into a staging folder (/DeveloperAI/Generated/) and let the user manually move files.  
   • Provide a diff viewer before overwriting.

4. Security  
   • Validate any code or asset created by the AI – lint, unit test, run static analysis.

────────────────────────────────────────
5. Usage checklist
────────────────────────────────────────
☐ Build / recompile the plug-ins (Unreal) or trigger “Reimport” (Unity).  
☐ Add your DEVELOPER_AI_KEY as an environment variable or via editor preferences.  
☐ In the editor, open the new UI (“Developer AI” toolbar button or menu).  
☐ Prompt the AI, review generated scripts/Blueprints, iterate.  
☐ Commit only reviewed files to VCS.

Notes: use non-blocking delegates, write to Content/DeveloperAI/Generated/, and register imported files with FAssetRegistryModule. Below is a pragmatic, copy-paste-ready skeleton for DAIBlueprintLibrary.cpp that does async HTTP and writes a .txt stub into the staging folder. It shows how to handle response delegates and expose a Blueprint-callable async wrapper if needed.

// DAIBlueprintLibrary.cpp (improved async, simplified for sketch)
#include "DAIBlueprintLibrary.h"
#include "HttpModule.h"
#include "Interfaces/IHttpResponse.h"
#include "Misc/FileHelper.h"
#include "Misc/Paths.h"
#include "AssetRegistryModule.h"
#include "HAL/PlatformFilemanager.h"

FString GetStagingPath()
{
    // Content folder path (project-relative)
    return FPaths::Combine(FPaths::ProjectContentDir(), TEXT("DeveloperAI/Generated"));
}

FString SaveResponseToStaging(const FString& FileName, const FString& Content)
{
    IPlatformFile& PlatformFile = FPlatformFileManager::Get().GetPlatformFile();
    FString Dir = GetStagingPath();
    if (!PlatformFile.DirectoryExists(*Dir))
    {
        PlatformFile.CreateDirectoryTree(*Dir);
    }

    FString FilePath = FPaths::Combine(Dir, FileName);
    if (FFileHelper::SaveStringToFile(Content, *FilePath))
    {
        // Notify asset registry for text-based assets (so user sees it in Content Browser)
        FAssetRegistryModule::AssetCreated(nullptr); // placeholder - for non-UObject files, we rely on Refresh
        FPlatformProcess::Sleep(0.01f); // tiny wait to allow file system sync
        return FilePath;
    }
    return FString();
}

// Simple async request that writes response text to staging and returns path on completion.
// In production, replace text storage with Blueprint asset / .uasset creation via FAssetToolsModule
void UDAIBlueprintLibrary::RequestBlueprintStubAsync(const FString& Prompt, TFunction<void(bool, FString)> Callback)
{
    const FString Endpoint = TEXT("https://api.developerai.com/v1/generate/bp");

    TSharedRef<IHttpRequest, ESPMode::ThreadSafe> Request = FHttpModule::Get().CreateRequest();
    Request->SetURL(Endpoint);
    Request->SetVerb(TEXT("POST"));
    Request->SetHeader(TEXT("Content-Type"), TEXT("application/json"));

    FString Key = FPlatformMisc::GetEnvironmentVariable(TEXT("DEVELOPER_AI_KEY"));
    if (!Key.IsEmpty())
    {
        Request->SetHeader(TEXT("Authorization"), TEXT("Bearer ") + Key);
    }
    else
    {
        // Try project settings or return error
        Callback(false, TEXT("Missing DEVELOPER_AI_KEY environment variable"));
        return;
    }

    FString Body = FString::Printf(TEXT("{\"prompt\":\"%s\"}"), *Prompt.ReplaceEscapedCharWithChar());
    Request->SetContentAsString(Body);

    Request->OnProcessRequestComplete().BindLambda([Callback](FHttpRequestPtr Req, FHttpResponsePtr Resp, bool bWasSuccessful)
    {
        if (!bWasSuccessful || !Resp.IsValid())
        {
            Callback(false, TEXT("HTTP request failed."));
            return;
        }

        FString Content = Resp->GetContentAsString();

        // Basic validation: ensure response not empty
        if (Content.IsEmpty())
        {
            Callback(false, TEXT("Empty AI response"));
            return;
        }

        // Save to staging as .txt (replace with actual .uasset creation later)
        FString SafeName = TEXT("BPStub_") + FDateTime::UtcNow().ToString() + TEXT(".txt");
        FString SavedPath = SaveResponseToStaging(SafeName, Content);
        if (SavedPath.IsEmpty())
        {
            Callback(false, TEXT("Failed to save file to staging folder"));
            return;
        }

        // Inform caller with the path to the generated file
        Callback(true, SavedPath);
    });

    Request->ProcessRequest();
}

Improved Unity skeleton — better UX + cancellation
Key points: use EditorCoroutineUtility (2021+), HttpClient with cancellation token, EditorUtility.DisplayProgressBar, and staged file save under Assets/DeveloperAI/Generated/.

// DeveloperAIWindow.cs (important parts)
using System.Net.Http;
using System.Text;
using System.Threading;
using UnityEditor;
using Unity.EditorCoroutines.Editor;
using UnityEngine;

public class DeveloperAIWindow : EditorWindow
{
    private string prompt = "Describe your behaviour...";
    private CancellationTokenSource cts;

    [MenuItem("Tools/Developer AI %#d")]
    public static void ShowWindow() => GetWindow<DeveloperAIWindow>("Developer AI");

    private async void GenerateMonoBehaviour()
    {
        if (cts != null) { Debug.Log("Already running"); return; }
        cts = new CancellationTokenSource();

        const string endpoint = "https://api.developerai.com/v1/generate/csharp";
        using var client = new HttpClient();
        string key = System.Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable("DEVELOPER_AI_KEY");
        if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(key)) client.DefaultRequestHeaders.Add("Authorization", "Bearer " + key);

        try
        {
            var content = new StringContent("{\"prompt\":\"" + prompt + "\"}", Encoding.UTF8, "application/json");
            var response = await client.PostAsync(endpoint, content, cts.Token);

            if (!response.IsSuccessStatusCode)
            {
                Debug.LogError($"Developer AI error: {response.StatusCode} {response.ReasonPhrase}");
                return;
            }

            string csharp = await response.Content.ReadAsStringAsync();

            string folder = "Assets/DeveloperAI/Generated";
            System.IO.Directory.CreateDirectory(folder);
            string path = EditorUtility.SaveFilePanelInProject("Save Generated Script", "GeneratedBehaviour.cs", "cs", "");
            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(path)) path = System.IO.Path.Combine(folder, "GeneratedBehaviour_" + System.DateTime.UtcNow.ToString("yyyyMMddHHmmss") + ".cs");

            System.IO.File.WriteAllText(path, csharp);
            AssetDatabase.ImportAsset(path);
            EditorUtility.DisplayDialog("DeveloperAI", "Script generated to: " + path, "OK");
        }
        catch (System.OperationCanceledException)
        {
            Debug.Log("Generation cancelled.");
        }
        finally
        {
            cts.Dispose();
            cts = null;
            EditorUtility.ClearProgressBar();
            AssetDatabase.Refresh();
        }
    }

    private void OnDisable()
    {
        cts?.Cancel();
        cts?.Dispose();
        cts = null;
    }
}

One more helpful tip (because I can’t help myself)
If you want truly useful AI-generated Blueprints, design a small JSON schema for the Blueprint AST you want back now — nodes, pins, types — then have the AI output only JSON matching that schema. That lets you programmatically reconstruct Blueprints node-by-node with zero guesswork. You’ll thank me later; your artists will stop screaming at you.
